Appendix B
Meta Theory and Proofs for
Met

We provide meta theory and proofs for
Met
in

Sections

4
and
6
including all extensions.

B.1.
The Double Category of Effects

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{F}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\leqslant}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\leqslant}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{F^{\prime}}}

Figure 10
.
2-cells in a 2-category compared to 2-cells in a double category.

A double category extends a 2-category with an additional kind of morphisms.
Alongside the regular morphisms, now called
horizontal
morphisms,
there are also
vertical
morphisms that connect the objects of the 2-category.
This makes it possible to generalise the 2-cells to transform arbitrary morphisms,
whose source and target are connected by vertical morphisms.

Figure

10
shows the differences between 2-cells in a
2-category and those in a double category using syntax of
Met
.

In
Met
, objects/modes are given by effect contexts, the
horizontal morphisms by modalities, the vertical morphisms by the
sub-effecting relation, and 2-cells by the modality transformations.

Now we show that it indeed has the structure of a double category.

Since the sub-effecting relation is a preorder, effect contexts
(objects)
E
and sub-effecting (vertical morphisms)
E\leqslant F

obviously form a category given by the poset.

We repeat the definition of modalities and modality composition
from
Section

4.3
here for easy reference.
We define them directly in terms of morphisms between modes.

\begin{array}[]{rcr@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}[E]_{F}&:&E&\to&F\\
{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F}&:&D+(F-L)&\to&F\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rclcll}[E^{\prime}]_{F}&\circ&[E]_{E^{\prime}}&=&[E]_{F}\\
{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F}&\circ&[E]_{D+(F-L)}&=&[E]_{F}\\
[E]_{F}&\circ&{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{E}&=&[D+(E-L)]_{F}\\
{\langle{L_{1}}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D_{1}\rangle}_{F}&\circ&{\langle{L_{2}}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D_{2}\rangle}_{D_{1}+(F-L_{1})}&=&{\langle{L_{1}+L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D_{2}+D\rangle}_{F}&\text{ where }(L,D)=L_{2}\bowtie D_{1}\\
\end{array}

The effect contexts (objects) and modalities (horizontal morphisms)
also form a category since modality composition possesses
associativity and identity.
We have the following lemma.

Lemma B.1 (Modes and modalities form a category).

Modes and modalities form a category with the identity morphism

{\langle\rangle}_{E}={\langle\rangle}{}_{E}:E\to E
and the morphism composition

\mu_{F}\circ\nu_{F^{\prime}}
such that

(1)

Identity:
{\langle\rangle}_{F}\circ\mu_{F}=\mu_{F}=\mu_{F}\circ{\langle\rangle}_{E}
for
\mu_{F}:E\to F
.

(2)

Associativity:
(\mu_{E_{1}}\circ\nu_{E_{2}})\circ\xi_{E_{3}}=\mu_{E_{1}}\circ(\nu_{E_{2}}\circ\xi_{E_{3}})

for
\mu_{E_{1}}:E_{2}\to E_{1}
,
\nu_{E_{2}}:E_{3}\to E_{2}
, and
\xi_{E_{3}}:E\to E_{3}
.

Proof.

By inlining the definitions of modalities and checking each case.
∎

In
Section

4
, we only define the modality
transformations of shape
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}
where the targets of
\mu

and
\nu
are required to be the same effect context
F
.
This is enough for presenting the calculus, but we can further extend
it to allow
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F^{\prime}}
where
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
.
This is used in the meta theory for
Met
such as the
lock weakening lemma (
Lemma

B.11
.3).

The extended modality transformation relation is defined by the
transitive closure of the following rules.
Compared to the definition in
Section

4.3
, the only new rule is
MT-Mono
.

\frac{{\mu}_{F}:E^{\prime}\to F\quad\quad E\leqslant E^{\prime}\quad}{[E]_{F}\Rightarrow{\mu}_{F}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Abs}}}\par\frac{D\leqslant D^{\prime}}{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F}\Rightarrow{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D^{\prime}\rangle}_{F}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Upcast}}}\par\frac{(F-L)\equiv\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B,E}{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F}\Rightarrow{\langle{\ell,L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D,\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\rangle}_{F}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Expand}}}\par\frac{(F-L)\equiv\ell:P,E}{{\langle{\ell,L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D,\ell:P\rangle}_{F}\Rightarrow{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Shrink}}}\par\mathchoice{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\displaystyle\frac{F\leqslant F^{\prime}\quad}{\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu_{F^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Mono}}}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\textstyle\frac{F\leqslant F^{\prime}\hskip 8.19447pt}{\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu_{F^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Mono}}}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptstyle\frac{F\leqslant F^{\prime}\hskip 7.3612pt}{\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu_{F^{\prime}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Mono}}}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptscriptstyle\frac{F\leqslant F^{\prime}\hskip 7.3612pt}{\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu_{F^{\prime}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Mono}}}$}}}

The following lemmas shows that the transformation
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F^{\prime}}

satisfies the requirement of being 2-cells in the double category of
effects with well-defined vertical and horizontal composition.

Lemma B.2 (Modality transformations are 2-cells).

If
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F^{\prime}}
,
\mu_{F}:E\to F
, and
\nu_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
,
then
E\leqslant E^{\prime}
and
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
.
Moreover, the transformation relation is closed under vertical and
horizontal composition as shown by the following admissible rules.

\frac{\mu_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\nu_{F_{2}}\quad\nu_{F_{2}}\Rightarrow\xi_{F_{3}}}{\mu_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\xi_{F_{3}}}\par\frac{\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}\quad\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}\quad\mu_{F}:E\to F\quad\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}\quad}{\mu_{F}\circ\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}\circ\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}}

Proof.

We take the transitive closure of the modality transformation rules.

\frac{{\mu}_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}\quad E\leqslant E^{\prime}\quad F\leqslant F^{\prime}\quad}{[E]_{F}\Rightarrow{\mu}_{F^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Abs}}}\par\frac{D\leqslant D^{\prime}\quad(F^{\prime}-L)\equiv D_{1},E_{1}\equiv D_{2},E_{2}\quad F\leqslant F^{\prime}\quad L_{1}=\mathsf{dom}(D_{1})\quad L_{2}=\mathsf{dom}(D_{2})\quad\mathsf{present}(D_{2})\quad}{{\langle{L_{1},L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D,D_{1}\rangle}_{F}\Rightarrow{\langle{L_{2},L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D^{\prime},D_{2}\rangle}_{F^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Rel}}}

The predicate
\mathsf{present}(D)
checks if all labels in
D
are present.
Vertical composition follows directly from the fact that we take the
transitive closure.
Horizontal compositions follows from a case analysis on shapes of
modalities being composed.
The most nontrivial case is when all of
\mu_{F}
,
\nu_{E}
,

\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}
, and
\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}
are relative modalities.
The key observation is that
MT-Rel
always expand or shrink the
mask and extension of a relative modality simultaneously.
∎

More on Relationships between
Met
and Multimodal Type Theory

In addition to extending to a double category,
Met
also differs
from MTT in the usage of morphism families.
In types and terms we use
\mu
, indexed families of morphisms between
modes, instead of concrete morphisms
\mu_{F}
.
We do not lose any information. Given a typing judgement

\Gamma\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, the indexes for all modalities in
M
and
A

are determined by
E
.
Similarly, given a variable binding
x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A
, the indexes of
all modalities in
A
are determined by
\mu_{F}
.

Using indexed families of modalities in types and terms is very useful
to allow term variables to be used flexibly in different effect
contexts larger than where they are defined.
This greatly simplifies the support of subeffecting; we do not need to
update all indexes of modalities in a term or type when upcasting this
term or type to a larger effect context.
As a result, every type is always well-defined at any modes, which
means that we do not need to define the well-formedness judgement

A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
as in MTT.
Moreover, one important benefit of having types well-defined at any
modes is that when adding polymorphism for values, type quantifiers do not
need to carry the additional information about the modes at which the
type variables can be used, greatly simplifying the type system.
Otherwise, polymorphic types would need to have forms

\forall\alpha^{K\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}.A
, where
E
indicates the mode of
the type variable
\alpha
.

In contexts, we still keep concrete morphisms
\mu_{F}
, which makes the
proof trees of terms much more structured than using morphism
families.

B.2.
Lemmas for Modes and Modalities

Beyond the structure and properties of double categories shown in

Section

B.1
, we have some extra properties on
modes and modalities in
Met
.

The most important one is that horizontal morphisms (sub-effecting)
act functorially on vertical ones (modalities). In other words, the
action of
\mu
on effect contexts gives a total monotone function.

Lemma B.3 (Monotone modalities).

If
\mu_{F}:E\to F
and
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
, then

\mu_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
with
E\leqslant E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By definition.
∎

We prove the lemma on the equivalence between syntactic and semantic
definition of modality transformation in
Section

4.3
.
This lemma can be generalised to the general form of 2-cells in a
double category
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F^{\prime}}
where
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
.

See
4.1

Proof.

From left to right, it is obvious that the semantics is preserved
after taking the transitive closure. We only need to show the
transformation given by each rule satisfies the semantics.

Case

MT-Abs
. Follow from
Lemma

B.3
.

Case

MT-Upcast
. Since
D\leqslant D^{\prime}
, we have

D+(F-L)\leqslant D^{\prime}+(F-L)
for any
F
.

Case

MT-Expand
. Since
(F-L)\equiv\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B,E
, for any

F\leqslant F^{\prime}
we have
(F^{\prime}-L)\equiv\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B,E^{\prime}
for some
E^{\prime}
.
Both sides act on
F^{\prime}
give
D,\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B,E^{\prime}
.
Notice that it is important for
\ell
to not be absent here;
otherwise, in
F^{\prime}
we could upcast the absent type of
\ell

to any concrete operation arrows, which then breaks the condition

\mu(F^{\prime})\leqslant\nu(F^{\prime})
.

Case

MT-Shrink
. Similar to the above case.

From right to left, we need to show that for all pairs
\mu_{F}
and

\nu_{F}
satisfying the semantic definition, we have
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}

in the transitive closure of the syntactic rules.
This obviously holds for those transformation starting from absolute
modalities.
For those transformation starting from relative modalities, observe
that they can only be transformed to other relative modalities
according to the semantic definition.
By taking the transitive closure of the transformation rules for
relative modalities, we have

\frac{D\leqslant D^{\prime}\quad(F-L)\equiv D_{1},E_{1}\equiv D_{2},E_{2}\quad\quad L_{1}=\mathsf{dom}(D_{1})\quad L_{2}=\mathsf{dom}(D_{2})\quad\mathsf{present}(D_{2})\quad}{{\langle{L_{1},L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D,D_{1}\rangle}_{F}\Rightarrow{\langle{L_{2},L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D^{\prime},D_{2}\rangle}_{F}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Rel}}}

The predicate
\mathsf{present}(D)
checks if all labels in
D
are present.
Suppose
{\langle{L_{3}}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D_{3}\rangle}_{F}
and
{\langle{L_{4}}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D_{4}\rangle}_{F}
satisfies that

D_{3}+(F^{\prime}-L_{3})\leqslant D_{4}+(F^{\prime}-L_{4})
for all
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
.
We need to show that it is generated by
MT-Rel
.
The key observation is that for
{\langle{L_{3}}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D_{3}\rangle}_{F}
and

{\langle{L_{4}}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D_{4}\rangle}_{F}
to satisfy the semantic definition, we must have

L_{3}^{\prime}\equiv L_{4}^{\prime}
and
D_{3}^{\prime}\leqslant D_{4}^{\prime}
for
L_{3}\bowtie D_{3}=(L_{3}^{\prime},D_{3}^{\prime})
and
L_{4}\bowtie D_{4}=(L_{4}^{\prime},D_{4}^{\prime})
.
Otherwise, we can always construct counterexamples by choosing a
appropriate
F^{\prime}
.
Also notice that when
L_{3}
and
L_{4}
(
D_{3}
and
D_{4}
) are
different,
F
should provide enough labels to fill the gap.
Moreover, for all those labels in
D_{4}
but not in
D_{3}
, they
should be present to be stable under any
F^{\prime}
with
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
.
It is not hard to verify that
MT-Rel
covers all such pair of
relative modalities.

∎

Our proofs for type soundness and effect safety do not use ad-hoc case
analysis on shapes of modalities or rely on any specific properties
about the definition of composition and transformation
(except for the parts about effect handlers since they specify the
required modalities in the typing rules).
As a result, it should be possible to generalise our calculus and proofs
to other mode theories satisfying certain extra properties.
We state some properties of the mode theory as the following lemmas
for easier references in proofs. Most of them directly follow from the
definition.

Lemma B.4 (Vertical composition).

If
\mu_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\nu_{F_{2}}
and
\nu_{F_{2}}\Rightarrow\xi_{F_{3}}
, then
\mu_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\xi_{F_{3}}
.

Proof.

Follow from
Lemma

B.2

∎

Lemma B.5 (Horizontal composition).

If
\mu_{F}:E\to F
,
\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
,
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}
, and

\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}
, then
\mu_{F}\circ\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}\circ\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Follow from
Lemma

B.2

∎

Lemma B.6 (Monotone modality transformation).

If
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}
and
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
,
then
\mu_{F^{\prime}}\Rightarrow\nu_{F^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Follow from
Lemma

4.1

∎

Lemma B.7 (Asymmetric reflexivity of modality transformation).

If
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
and
\mu_{F}:E\to F
,
then
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu_{F^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By definition.
∎

B.3.
Lemmas for the Calculus

We prove structural and substitution lemmas for
Met
as well as some
other auxiliary lemmas for proving type soundness.

Lemma B.8 (Canonical forms).

1.

If
\,\vdash U:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is of shape
{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,U^{\prime}
.

2.

If
\,\vdash U:A\to B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is of shape
\lambda x^{A}.M
.

3.

If
\,\vdash U:\forall\alpha.A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is of shape
\Lambda\alpha.V
.

4.

If
\,\vdash U:(A,B)\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is of shape
(U_{1},U_{2})
.

5.

If
\,\vdash U:A+B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is either of shape
{{\mathbsf{inl}}}\;U^{\prime}
or of shape
{{\mathbsf{inr}}}\;U^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Directly follows from the typing rules.
∎

In order to define the lock weakening lemma, we first define a context
update operation
{\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}
which gives a new context
derived from updating the indexes of all locks and variable bindings
in
\Gamma
such that
\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):\_\to F^{\prime}
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\llparenthesis\cdot\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\cdot\\
{\llparenthesis\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[E]_{F^{\prime}}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[E]_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\\
{\llparenthesis\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F^{\prime}}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{D+(F-L)}\\
{\llparenthesis x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F^{\prime}}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F^{\prime}}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}\\
{\llparenthesis\alpha:K,\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\alpha:K,{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}\\
\end{array}

We have the following lemma showing that the index update operation
preserves the
\mathsf{locks}(-)
operation except for updating the index.

Lemma B.9 (Index update preserves composition).

If
\mu_{F}=\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma):E\to F
,
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
, and

\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
, then
\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}})=\mu_{F^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on the context and using the property
that
(\mu\circ\nu)_{F}=\mu_{F}\circ\nu_{E}
for
\mu_{F}:E\to F
.
∎

Corollary B.10 (Index update preserves transformation).

If
\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma):E\to F
,
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
, and

\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
, then
\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma)\Rightarrow\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

Immediately follow from
Lemma

B.9
and

Lemma

B.7
.
∎

We have the following structural lemmas.

Lemma B.11 (Structural rules).

The following structural rules are admissible.

1.

Variable weakening.

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

2.

Variable swapping.

\frac{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,y\@ifmtarg{\nu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}}}B,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:A^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma,y\@ifmtarg{\nu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}}}B,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:A^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

3.

Lock weakening.

\frac{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}\quad\nu_{F}:F^{\prime}\to F\quad\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

4.

Type variable weakening.

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma,\alpha:K,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

5.

Type variable swapping.

\frac{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\alpha:K,\Gamma_{3}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma_{1},\alpha:K,\Gamma_{3}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\par\frac{\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma_{2})\quad{\Gamma_{1},\alpha:K,\Gamma_{3}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}}{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\alpha:K,\Gamma_{3}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

Proof.

1, 2, 4, and 5 follow from straightforward induction on the typing derivation.
For 3, we also proceed by induction on the typing derivation.
The most interesting case is
T-Var
.
Other cases mostly follow from IHs.

Case

\frac{\nu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}=\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma_{2}):E\to F_{1}\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\nu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}\,(1)}\text{ or }\Gamma\vdash A:\mathsf{Abs}}{\Gamma_{1},x\@ifmtarg{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}}}},\Gamma_{2}\vdash x:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Var}}}

Trivial when
A
is pure. Otherwise, case analysis on where the lock
weakening happens.

Case

\Gamma
. Supposing
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma_{0}
and after lock weakening we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},\Gamma_{0}^{\prime},x\@ifmtarg{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}}}}},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}

where
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}={\llparenthesis\Gamma_{2}\rrparenthesis}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F_{1}^{\prime}
and

\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}={\llparenthesis\Gamma_{0}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}:F_{1}^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

B.9
on
\Gamma_{0}
,
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
, and

Lemma

B.3
, we have
F_{1}\leqslant F_{1}^{\prime}
.
Then by
(1)
and
Lemma

B.6
, we have

\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}\Rightarrow\nu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}
.
Then by
Lemma

B.9
we have
\nu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}=\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
.
Finally by
T-Var
we have

{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},\Gamma_{0}^{\prime},x\@ifmtarg{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}}}}},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash x:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

Case

\Gamma_{2}
. Suppose
\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{0},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}
.
is weakened to
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}=\Gamma_{0},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}
.
By
Corollary

B.10
we have

\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime})\Rightarrow\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}})
.
Then by
Lemma

B.5
we have we have

\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma_{2})\Rightarrow\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
.
By
Lemma

B.4
and
(1)
, we have
\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
.
Finally by
T-Var
we have

{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}}}},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash x:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

Case

\frac{\mu^{\prime}_{E}:F_{1}\to E\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu^{\prime}_{E}}}}\vdash V:A\;{{@}}\,{{F_{1}}}}\,(1)}}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\,V:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Mod}}}

We have

{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{E}}}}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}={\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}},{\llparenthesis\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{E}}}}\rrparenthesis}_{E^{\prime}}={\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}}}}.

Supposing
\mu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}:F_{1}^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}
, by

\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}}}}):F_{1}^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
and
IH on
(1)
, we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}}}}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F_{1}^{\prime}}}.

Then by
T-Mod
we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\,V:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}.

Case

\frac{\nu^{\prime}_{E}:F_{1}\to E\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\nu^{\prime}_{E}}}}\vdash V:{{{\mu^{\prime}}}}A\;{{@}}\,{{F_{1}}}}\,(1)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime},x\@ifmtarg{\nu^{\prime}_{E}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}{:}{:_{{{\nu^{\prime}_{E}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}}}}A\vdash M:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(2)}}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\@ifmtarg{\nu^{\prime}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu^{\prime}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\;x=V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Letmod}}}

By IH on
(1)
, we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}}}}\vdash V:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F_{1}^{\prime}}}

where
\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}:F_{1}^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}
.
By IH on
(2)
, we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}},x\@ifmtarg{\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}}}}}A\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}.

Then by
T-Letmod
, we have

{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}\vdash\@ifmtarg{\nu^{\prime}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu^{\prime}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\;x=V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

Case

\frac{\nu^{\prime}_{E}:F_{1}\to E\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\nu^{\prime}_{E}}}},\overline{\alpha:K}\vdash V:{{{\mu^{\prime}}}}A\;{{@}}\,{{F_{1}}}}\,(1)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime},x\@ifmtarg{\nu^{\prime}_{E}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}{:}{:_{{{\nu^{\prime}_{E}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}}}}\forall\overline{\alpha^{K}}.A\vdash M:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(2)}}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\@ifmtarg{\nu^{\prime}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu^{\prime}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\;\Lambda\overline{\alpha^{K}}.x=V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Letmod'}}}

Similar to the case for
T-Letmod
.
BY IH on
(1)
, we have

{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}}}},\overline{\alpha:K}\vdash V:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F_{1}^{\prime}}}}

where
\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}:F_{1}^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}
. By IH on
(2)
, we have

{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}},x\@ifmtarg{\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}^{\prime}}}}}}\forall\overline{\alpha^{K}}.A\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}.

Then by
T-Letmod’
, we have

{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}\vdash\@ifmtarg{\nu^{\prime}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu^{\prime}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\;\Lambda\overline{\alpha^{K}}.x=V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

Case

T-TAbs
,
T-Abs
,
T-TApp
,
T-App
,

T-Do
,
T-Mask
,
T-Handler
,
T-ModAbs
, other
handlers and data types. Follow from IH. Similar to other cases we
have shown.

∎

As a corollary of
Lemma

B.11
.3, the following
sub-effecting rule is admissible.

Corollary B.12 (Sub-effecting).

The following rule is admissible.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma):E\to F\quad F\leqslant F^{\prime}\quad\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}\quad}{{\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

Proof.

Follow from
Lemma

B.11
.3 by adding the lock

\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[F]_{\cdot}}}}
to the left of
\Gamma
in

\Gamma\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, and weaken it to

\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[F^{\prime}]_{\cdot}}}}
.
Note that typing judgements still hold after adding a lock to or
removing a lock from the left of the context, as long as the new
contexts are still well-defined.
∎

The following lemma reflects the intuition that pure values can be
used in any effect context.

Lemma B.13 (Pure Promotion).

The following promotion rule is admissible.

\frac{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad\Gamma_{1}\vdash A:\mathsf{Abs}\quad\quad\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma):E\to F\quad\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime}):E^{\prime}\to F\quad\mathsf{fv}(V)\cap\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})=\emptyset}{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation of
V
.

Case

T-Var
. Trivial.

Case

\frac{\mu_{E}:F_{1}\to E\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{E}}}}\vdash V:A\;{{@}}\,{{F_{1}}}}\,(1)}}{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,V:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Mod}}}

Case analysis on the shape of
\mu
.

Case

\mu
is relative. By kinding,
A
is also pure.
By IH on
(1)
, we have

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{E^{\prime}}}}}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F_{1}^{\prime}}}

where
\mu_{E^{\prime}}:F_{1}^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}
.
Then by
T-Mod
we have

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,V:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}

Case

\mu
is absolute. We have
\mu=[F_{1}]
and

\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{E^{\prime}}}}})=[F_{1}]_{F}=\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{E}}}})
.
Thus, replacing the context
(\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{E}}}})
with

(\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{E^{\prime}}}}})
in
(1)
does not influence all
usages of
T-Var
in the derivation tree of
(1)
. We have

{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{E^{\prime}}}}}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F_{1}}}}

Then by
T-Mod
we have

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,V:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}

Case

T-TAbs
. Follow from IH and

Lemma

B.11
.5.

Case

T-Abs
. Impossible since function types are impure.

Case

Data types. Follow from IHs.

∎

Lemma B.14 (Substitution).

The following substitution rules are admissible.

1.

Preservation of kinds under type substitution.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash A:K\quad\Gamma,\alpha:K,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash B:K^{\prime}}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash B[A/\alpha]:K^{\prime}}

2.

Preservation of types under type substitution.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash A:K\quad\Gamma,\alpha:K,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M[A/\alpha]:B[A/\alpha]\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

3.

Preservation of types under value substitution.

\frac{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F^{\prime}}}\quad\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M[V/x]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

Proof.

1. By straightforward induction on the kinding derivation.

2. By straightforward induction on the typing derivation of
M
.

3. By induction on the typing derivation of
M
.
Trivial when variable
x
is not used. In the following induction we
always assume
x
is used.

Case

\frac{\nu_{F}=\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime}):E\to F\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}}\,(1)}\text{ or }\Gamma\vdash A:\mathsf{Abs}}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash x:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Var}}}

Case analysis on the purity of
A

Case

Impure. By
\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F^{\prime}}}
,

(1)
, and
Lemma

B.11
.3, we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}.

Then, by context equivalence,
Lemma

B.11
.1, and

Lemma

B.11
.4, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}.

Case

Pure. By
\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F^{\prime}}}
and

Lemma

B.13
, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}.

Case

\frac{\mu^{\prime}_{E}:F_{1}\to E\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu^{\prime}_{E}}}}\vdash W:B\;{{@}}\,{{F_{1}}}}\,(1)}}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\,W:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}}}}B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Mod}}}

By IH on
(1)
we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}_{E}}}}\vdash W[V/x]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F_{1}}}.

Then by
T-Mod
we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\,W)[V/x]:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}}}}B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}

Case

\frac{\nu_{E}:F_{1}\to E\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\nu_{E}}}}\vdash W:{{{\mu^{\prime}}}}A^{\prime}\;{{@}}\,{{F_{1}}}}\,(1)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},y\@ifmtarg{\nu_{E}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}{:}{:_{{{\nu_{E}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}}}}A^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(2)}}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\@ifmtarg{\nu}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\;y=W\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Letmod}}}

By IH on
(1)
, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{E}}}}\vdash W[V/x]:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu^{\prime}}}}A^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F_{1}}}.

T-Letmod
, we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash(\@ifmtarg{\nu}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\;y=W\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M)[V/x]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}

Case

\frac{\nu_{E}:F_{1}\to E\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\nu_{E}}}},\overline{\alpha:K}\vdash V:{{{\mu^{\prime}}}}A^{\prime}\;{{@}}\,{{F_{1}}}}\,(1)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},y\@ifmtarg{\nu_{E}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}{:}{:_{{{\nu_{E}\circ\mu^{\prime}_{F_{1}}}}}}\forall\overline{\alpha^{K}}.A^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(2)}}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\@ifmtarg{\nu}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\;\Lambda\overline{\alpha^{K}}.y=V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Letmod'}}}

Similar to the case for
T-Letmod
. Our goal follows from IH on

(1)
, IH on

, and
T-Letmod’
.

Case

\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\!}\rangle}_{E}}}}\vdash M:B\;{{@}}\,{{E-L}}}\,(1)}\quad}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{mask}}}_{L}\;M:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\!}\rangle}}}}B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Mask}}}

(1)
we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\!}\rangle}_{E}}}}\vdash M[V/x]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E-L}}.

we have

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash({{\mathbsf{mask}}}_{L}\;M)[V/x]:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\!}\rangle}}}}B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}

Case

\frac{{D=\{\ell_{i}:A_{i}\twoheadrightarrow B_{i}\}_{i}}\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{{\langle{\!\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{E}}}}\vdash M:A_{0}\;{{@}}\,{{D+E}}}\,(1)}\quad\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},y:{{{{\langle{\!\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}}}}A_{0}\vdash N:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(2)}\quad[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime},p_{i}:A_{i},{r_{i}}:B_{i}\to B\vdash N_{i}:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(3)}]_{i}}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;y\mapsto N\}\uplus\{\ell_{i}\;p_{i}\;r_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}_{i}:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handler}}}

Follow from IH on
(1)
,
(2)
,
(3)
, and reapplying

T-Handler
.

Case

T-TAbs
,
T-TApp
,
T-Abs
,
T-App
,

T-Do
. Follow from IH.

Case

T-ModAbs
, other handlers and data types. Follow from IH.

∎

B.4.
Progress

See
4.3

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
. The most
non-trivial cases are
T-Mask
and
T-Handler
. Other cases
follow from IHs and reduction rules, using

Lemma

B.8
.

Case

M
is in a value normal form
U
. Trivial. Base case.

Case

T-Do
. Trivial. Base case.

Case

T-Mod
.
{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,V
. By IH on
V
.

Case

T-Letmod
.
\@ifmtarg{\nu}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\;x=V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N
. By IH on

V
, if
V
is reducible then
M
is reducible; otherwise,
V
is in a
value normal form, then by
Lemma

B.8
we have that

M
is reducible by
E-Letmod
.

Case

T-Letmod’
. Similar to the case for
T-Letmod
.

Case

T-TApp
.
M\,A
. Similarly by IH on
M
,
Lemma

B.8
, and
E-TApp
.

Case

T-App
.
M\,N
. Similarly by IH on
M
and
N
,
Lemma

B.8
, and
E-App
.

Case

T-Mask
.
{{\mathbsf{mask}}}^{E}\,M
. By IH on
M
.

Case

M
is reducible. Trivial.

Case

M
is in a value normal form. By
E-Mask
.

Case

M=\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\,U]
with
n{-}\mathsf{free}(\ell,\mathcal{E})
. The
whole term is in a normal form.

Case

Handlers. The general form is
{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\delta}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H
. By IH on
M
.

Case

M
is reducible. Trivial.

Case

M
is in a value normal form. By
E-Ret
.

Case

M=\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;U]
with
n{-}\mathsf{free}(\ell,\mathcal{E})
. If

n=0
and
\ell\in H
, then reducible by
E-Op
. Otherwise,
the whole term is in a normal form.

Case

T-ModAbs
.
{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[]}\,M
. If
M\leadsto N
,
follow by IH on
M
. Otherwise,
M
must be in a value normal form
because the
T-ModAbs
requires
M
to have the empty effect. In
this case,
{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[]}\,M
is also in a value normal form.

Case

Other handlers and data types. Similar to other cases.

∎

B.5.
Subject Reduction

See
4.4

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Gamma\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
.

Case

T-Var
. Impossible as there is no further reduction.

Case

\frac{\mu_{F}:E\to F\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}}\vdash V:A\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(1)}}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,V:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Mod}}}

The only way to reduce is by

and

. IH on

(1)
gives

{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,W:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}.

Case

\frac{\nu_{F}:E\to F\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\nu_{F}}}}\vdash V:{{{\mu}}}A\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(1)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\nu_{F}\circ\mu_{E}}{:}{:_{{{\nu_{F}\circ\mu_{E}}}}}A\vdash M:B\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(2)}}{\Gamma\vdash\@ifmtarg{\nu}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\;x=V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Letmod}}}

By case analysis on the reduction.

Case

E-Lift
with
V\leadsto W
. By IH on
(1)
and
reapplying
T-Letmod
.

Case

E-Letmod
. We have
V={{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,U
and

\@ifmtarg{\nu}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\;x={{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,U\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M\leadsto M[U/x].

(1)
gives

{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{E}}}}\vdash U:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}.

where
\mu_{E}:E^{\prime}\to E
.
By context equivalence, we have

{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}\circ\mu_{E}}}}\vdash U:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

where
\nu_{F}\circ\mu_{E}:E^{\prime}\to F

B.14
.3 and
(2)
, we have

{\Gamma\vdash M[U/x]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}.

Case

\frac{\nu_{F}:E\to F\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\nu_{F}}}},\overline{\alpha:K}\vdash V:{{{\mu}}}A\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(1)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\nu_{F}\circ\mu_{E}}{:}{:_{{{\nu_{F}\circ\mu_{E}}}}}\forall\overline{\alpha^{K}}.A\vdash M:B\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(2)}}{\Gamma\vdash\@ifmtarg{\nu}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}_{\nu}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\;\Lambda\overline{\alpha^{K}}.x=V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Letmod'}}}

,
T-Abs
. Impossible as there is no further reduction.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma\vdash M:\forall\alpha^{K}.B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(1)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma\vdash A:K}\,(2)}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash M\,A:B[A/\alpha]\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-TApp}}}

\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\!}\rangle}_{F}}}}\vdash M:A\;{{@}}\,{{F-L}}}\,(1)}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{mask}}}_{L}\;M:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle{L}{\mkern 1.0mu\mid\!}\rangle}}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Mask}}}

\frac{H=\{{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto N\}\uplus\{\ell_{i}\;p_{i}\;r_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}_{i}\quad\quad{D=\{\ell_{i}:A_{i}\twoheadrightarrow B_{i}\}_{i}}\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{{\langle{\!\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F}}}}\vdash M:A\;{{@}}\,{{D+F}}}\,(1)}\quad\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,x:{{{{\langle{\!\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}}}}A\vdash N:B\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(2)}\quad[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\Gamma,p_{i}:A_{i},{r_{i}}:B_{i}\to B\vdash N_{i}:B\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(3)}]_{i}}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handler}}}

E-Op
. We have
M=\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell_{j}\;U]
,

0{-}\mathsf{free}(\ell_{j},\mathcal{E})
,
\ell_{j}\,p_{j}\,r_{j}\mapsto N_{j}

{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle{\!\mid\mkern 1.0mu}D\rangle}_{F}}}}\vdash U:A_{j}\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{D+F}}}.

A_{j}
is pure and

, we have

.

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\Gamma\vdash U:A_{j}\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(4)}

being pure allows

to be accessed in any

and a straightforward induction on

and

we have

{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{\Gamma\vdash\lambda y.{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;\mathcal{E}[y]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H:B_{j}\to B\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(5)}.

(3)
,

,
(5)
, and

.3 we have

\frac{{D=\{\ell_{i}:A_{i}\twoheadrightarrow B_{i}\}_{i}}\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{[D+E]_{F}}}}\vdash M:A\;{{@}}\,{{D+E}}}\,(1)}\quad\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{[E]_{F}}}},x:{{{[D+E]}}}A\vdash N:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(2)}\quad[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{[E]_{F}}}},p_{i}:A_{i},r_{i}:{{{[E]}}}(B_{i}\to B)\vdash N_{i}:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(3)}]_{i}\quad{[E]_{F}\Rightarrow{\langle\rangle}_{F}}}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\spadesuit}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto N\}\uplus\{\ell_{i}\;p_{i}\;r_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}_{i}:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handler${}^{\spadesuit}$}}}

{[E]_{F}\Rightarrow{\langle\rangle}_{F}}

.3 we have

\Gamma\vdash N[({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[D+E]}\,U)/x]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}.

E-Op
♠
.

M=\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell_{j}\;U]
,

0{-}\mathsf{free}(\ell_{j},\mathcal{E})
,

is well-kinded,

and

are pure.
